Independent research • 9 November 2026 • Research working paper

Do analysts underforecast the first rare-disease DMT?

There is a credible early enzyme-replacement underforecasting cluster. The evidence does not support a general first-DMT sales premium—or a conclusion that CMP-002 is undervalued.

This reconstruction begins with regulatory approvals and clinical comparability. The supplied analysis remains useful context; it did not supply the inclusion list or determine which outcomes to keep. Because earlier results were already known, this is source-independent research, not a blinded experiment.

1,442retrieved regulatory rows with explicit screen dispositions
82primary-period candidates, boundaries and adjacent therapies
20structured analogue dossiers, including missing-data and immature cases
5new preapproval target-year observations; 0 fully matched cumulative curves
FindingsMethodUniverseNew evidenceAnalogue selectionCase dossiersCMP-002 implicationsInvestor claimsMissing dataSources

What changed in the independent pass

The best new lead is an original January 2004 publication by SG Cowen analyst Yaron Werber. It contains forecasts for three then-unapproved enzyme therapies. All three subsequently exceeded those quoted 2007 figures. Their shared author, vintage and therapeutic family make this a valuable cluster to investigate, rather than three independent confirmations of an industry-wide bias.

New counterevidence makes the story more discriminating. Vimizim's quoted 2017 estimate was almost exactly realized. Kanuma's quoted 2020 estimate was far above actual sales; its sponsor later explicitly reduced patient-growth assumptions. Hidden prevalence can create upside, but it can also remain commercially inaccessible.

Clinical relevance and forecast measurability point to different cases. Spinraza and Brineura are the strongest mature route/CNS comparators. Older enzyme therapies provide better newly recovered forecast evidence. Ztalmy and Daybue belong in a separate neurodevelopmental commercial cohort. Nulibry, Lenmeldy and Kebilidi help test the age/function window without supplying a mature normalized sales-bias estimate.

Decision today: use the case evidence to specify CMP-002's diagnosis, eligibility, procedure capacity, exposure, competition and net-price sensitivities. Do not apply a historical “rare-disease underforecasting” multiplier. The current evidence can shape model structure; it cannot calibrate a universal uplift.

New forecast source: Yaron Werber (SG Cowen), Lysosomal storage diseases market, January 2004. Countercases: Vimizim contemporaneous quotation; Kanuma contemporaneous quotation; Alexion patient-growth reassessment.

The question, estimand and research pipeline

The target is signed error in preapproval, approval-conditional net product-sales forecasts among launched first rare-disease DMTs. It is not approval-probability calibration, forecast revision, market capitalization performance or the value of all assets at a company. The decision is whether defensible historical mechanisms should change an independent CMP-002 sales model.

A “first approved DMT” requires a disease-targeted mechanism and launch-time evidence of changing disease course or pathophysiology. First approved drug, first disease-specific symptomatic treatment, first-in-class, first gene therapy and first available disease-modifying care are different categories. Prior diet, transplantation and off-label DMT are recorded explicitly. Investigational exposure during the medicine's own development is a launch-bridge issue, not automatic disqualification.

StepWhat was done and whyPrecision / access tradeoff
Recover existing workRead the original extensive brief, protocol, source audit and earlier findings; preserve rather than overwrite them.Avoids repeated failed searches; prior knowledge means selection cannot be called blinded.
Build a source-independent frameRead official CDER original-approval workbook and current CBER cell/gene roster; retain every retrieved row and reason.Broad, low-cost structured coverage; original-NME and current-roster boundaries prevent a complete orphan census.
Freeze relevance, then retrieve forecastsPrioritize biology, age/function window, diagnosis, delivery and exposure before new commercial extraction.Qualitative dimensions are more defensible than an outcome-fitted numerical similarity score.
Verify numerical evidenceUse contemporaneous analyst writing or dated quotations, then SEC/company product-revenue disclosures.Original annual models are best but access-limited; quotations provide usable leads with explicit missing scope.
Transform only supported inputsCalculate raw target-year differences; preserve unknown PoS, geography and revenue owner; no peak-to-curve conversion.Reproducible arithmetic does not establish conditionality, causation or independent validation.
Synthesize and stop at access boundaryRetain missing forecasts, neutral outcomes and disappointing cases; identify exact model requests.More generic peak-sales searches would add weak proxies. No paid collection, bulk scraping or extensive fitting was undertaken.

Why use cumulative sales—and which cumulative sales?

Keep first 36 commercial months as the primary endpoint and 60 months as the secondary endpoint. Cumulative sales capture launch delays, backlog conversion and continuation better than an isolated peak. A short window may mistake a delayed ramp for a small market; a long window becomes more exposed to competition, label changes and pricing. Report both when data permit.

Use two clocks: actual launch-aligned exposure for commercial uptake, and the originally forecast calendar timeline for investor cash-flow timing. A delayed approval should appear in the timing comparison rather than vanish through rebasing. Annual trajectories identify offsetting early beats and later misses. Lifetime cumulative sales are too assumption-dependent for the primary retrospective test.

Select the forecast closest to 12 months before approval within a 9–15-month window for a standardized primary comparison; keep the latest preapproval observation and earlier-stage forecasts as separate sensitivity strata. The user's broader preapproval question is preserved by those strata. None of the five new observations falls in the primary vintage window.

Match the original disease, expected age/genotype scope, territory, currency, revenue owner and product-sales basis. Add later territories only in a separately matched expansion analysis. Do not use later diseases to explain a beat. An age expansion within the same disease also needs a flag and allocation. A royalty is not the licensee's end-market revenue; list price is not net revenue.

PoS normalization and selection

Removing a documented probability multiplier is appropriate for the commercial question. If a model already shows conditional sales and applies PoS only in valuation, dividing its sales again is wrong. Unknown probability treatment remains unknown. A price target, discount rate or development stage cannot identify the missing multiplier.

Conditioning on approved products is consistent with this study's stated estimand; it does not answer whether investors correctly price all preclinical assets. Normalizing PoS does not fix selective availability of successful-launch reports, hindsight selection of analogues, choosing the most favorable analyst, or loss of revenue disclosure after acquisitions. Those require a frozen universe, missingness ledger and symmetric retrieval rules.

If usable curves are obtained, compute A/F−1 and log(A/F), report median and distribution, and count drug–indication pairs rather than analyst notes. Separate within-drug analyst dispersion from across-drug variation. Equal-weight results should be primary; dollar weighting, forecast vintage, first-status boundaries, country scope and unchanged-label cases are sensitivities. No p-value or pooled bias estimate is calculated here because the matching gates are not met.

Methods: rebuild protocol · priority freeze · coverage and deviations.

A broader universe with visible boundaries

The CDER compilation contains 1,387 original approvals in 1985–2025. Of 924 approvals in the 2000–2025 primary period, 380 have at least one original orphan-designated use. Partial orphan designation was retained. The live CBER roster contributes 55 products, screened separately; its relevant original dates were checked on product pages.

The resulting clinical shortlist contains 27 first-approved-DMT candidates, 38 prior-care boundaries and 17 adjacent or DMT-uncertain therapies in the primary period. These are provisional clinical categories, not 82 confirmed eligible first DMTs. Thirteen pre-2000 precedents and four verified 2026 entrants are retained separately, making 99 extended candidate/control entries. The US orphan designation is used as an initial rarity proxy (normally fewer than 200,000 US patients, with a statutory cost-recovery exception); exception status and actual accessible population require separate adjudication.

Retrieved frame / dispositionRowsInterpretation

All other current CBER roster entries41Oncology, established alternatives or supportive/tissue interventions; some dates intentionally unadjudicated.
Full retrieved-frame disposition1,442No unexplained pending rows; provisional reasons are distinguishable from verified clinical adjudication.
This is not an exhaustive historical first-DMT census. The OOPD request returned a search form rather than a usable approval export. Original-NME data omit some repurposed drugs, first rare indications approved as supplements and non-NMEs. The current CBER list can omit historical withdrawals. The 544 non-orphan-designated original approvals were not all clinically adjudicated for rarity. These gaps prevent population-wide inference.

Representative omitted-route checks include Fintepla, Ravicti, Procysbi and Cystadrops. Most are likely symptomatic or followers, but that is not a justification for assuming every missing approval is ineligible. A full orphan export, non-NME/supplement reconciliation and historical biologics list are the right next route to complete the denominator.

The focus on chronic genetic/metabolic/CNS disease also excludes acute infections, supportive/diagnostic interventions and tumor settings. This goes beyond the original oncology exclusion and is disclosed as a scope narrowing. Withdrawn or disappointing products are not deleted for their outcomes: Oxbryta, Relyvrio and Adakveo remain visible as follower/control records.

Five new target-year observations

All amounts are nominal USD millions. Differences are raw actual / quoted forecast − 1. The actuals are worldwide recognized product sales; forecast geography is inferred from context, not fully documented. These are neither approval-conditional estimates nor matched 36-/60-month outcomes.

Drug–indicationForecast dateTarget yearForecastActualRaw differenceEvidence
Naglazyme / MPS VI2004-01200730.086.2+187.3%Forecast / actual
Myozyme / Pompe2004-01200765.0200.7+208.8%Forecast / actual
Elaprase / MPS II2004-01200730.0181.8+506.0%Forecast / actual
Vimizim / MPS IVA2013-11-212017416.0413.251-0.7%Forecast / actual
Kanuma / LAL deficiency2015-05-062020603.0117.9-80.4%Forecast / actual

What the three 2004 observations establish: an identifiable sell-side analyst published specific preapproval annual expectations that were below later reported product sales. They improve on unsourced peak anecdotes. They do not establish how many other analysts agreed, whether the estimates were probability-weighted, or whether the expected territory/age mix matched actual sales.

What the countercases establish: first-entry ERT status is compatible with an approximately realized expectation or a major shortfall. Vimizim's estimate was made much closer to approval than the 2004 cluster. Kanuma's number was attributed to Cortellis rather than a named sell-side analyst. Differences in vintage and source type are reasons to stratify, not reasons to discard unfavorable observations.

Kanuma's source called $603m a peak in a specified year, 2020. The comparison uses that stated target year; no annual curve was constructed. Vimizim's source said annual sales “by 2017,” treated here as a target-year quotation with that wording preserved. No later disease indication is needed to calculate these five checks, although expected age/phenotype and territory scope remain unresolved.

Probability sensitivity without inventing probabilities

If quoted F = p × conditional forecast, then conditional error = p × A/F − 1.
The break-even probability is p* = F/A.
2004 caseBreak-even scalar PoSInterpretation
Naglazyme34.8%Raw beat remains a conditional beat only if the true multiplier exceeds this threshold.
Myozyme32.4%Raw beat remains a conditional beat only if the true multiplier exceeds this threshold.
Elaprase16.5%Raw beat remains a conditional beat only if the true multiplier exceeds this threshold.

These thresholds are not estimates of the original models' PoS. For example, a hypothetical 30% multiplier would reverse the Naglazyme comparison despite its large raw beat. Conversely, if Kanuma's quoted estimate were risk-weighted by a scalar no greater than 100%, undoing that multiplier would make its conditional forecast larger and the shortfall more severe, assuming the other scope matches held.

Geography can also reverse the sign: Naglazyme's reported 2007 global revenue was $86.2m, but US revenue derives to $17.5m after subtracting $68.7m ex-US. That is why a worldwide actual cannot be casually matched to an unspecified forecast. The common 2004 publication also includes five already marketed products; all eight rows are retained in the extraction record rather than selecting only favorable preapproval outcomes.

What can and cannot be aggregated

Five new raw comparisons; three from one analyst publication; zero strict 36-month curves; zero strict 60-month curves. No pooled beat rate, median “bias,” confidence interval or uplift is reported. The five cases mix earlier-stage and near-approval vintages and several source types. A simple fraction above forecast would answer a question about these accessible excerpts, not a representative first-DMT cohort.

External forecasting research: useful context, different questions

SourceWhat is relevantWhy it does not establish this hypothesis
Deloitte / ISPOR 2024 abstract284 FDA-approved drugs/new indications, 2012–2021; rare-disease launches at small and midsize companies often met or exceeded expectations.Not first-DMT-only, not a disclosed normalized 36-month cohort; “meet or exceed” is not signed underforecasting. Case-level data and denominator definitions needed.
McKinsey 2024 first-time launcher analysisOnly 20–30% of first-time launchers exceeded expectations in the stated recent period, versus 40–50% of established launchers.First company launch is not first disease treatment. Excludes fully partnered assets and defines launchers with a sales threshold; cannot transfer the rate to CMP-002.
Cha et al., 2013Well-known original forecasting study identified in the bounded literature pass.Bibliographic record verified; full text was inaccessible here. No result is treated as independently re-extracted.

The first two studies can differ without contradiction: company populations, periods, thresholds and the distinction between “meet/exceed” and “exceed” differ. They motivate competing operational hypotheses, not a meta-analytic rare-disease bias estimate. The earlier 2026 rare-launch article lead remains access-limited and is not upgraded to verified full-text evidence.

Choose an analogue for an assumption

A single TAM number is an inadequate similarity measure. Compare eligible diagnosed patients, incident versus prevalent pools, age/function window, treatment frequency, clinical benefit, center capacity, payer mix, net price and competitive durability. A smaller IV enzyme market can be useful for patient finding while a larger intrathecal ASO market can be useful for procedure economics. Neither supplies an all-purpose forecast curve.

Efficacy should be described in patient-relevant dimensions: survival, seizure burden, functional gain or preservation, magnitude, onset, durability, safety/tolerability, response heterogeneity and evidence quality. Percent changes on unrelated scales cannot be normalized into a credible common efficacy score. Preclinical rescue supports biological plausibility and scenario design; it does not estimate human willingness to start, persistence or developmental reversibility.

Population-size matching remains incomplete. The 2004 analyst estimated worldwide affected populations of about 1,100 for MPS VI, 2,000 for MPS II and 5,000–10,000 for Pompe. Those are historical beliefs, not current eligible US markets. Ztalmy's company-reported >165 active patients at year-end2023 is a treatment count, not disease prevalence. These denominators cannot be ranked against a SYNGAP1 advocacy census as if they were equivalent TAMs. The data retain accessible US population as unknown until date, genotype, age, geography and eligibility are matched; no falsely precise TAM-distance score is assigned.

The 2004 trio is a commercial mechanism cluster, not the closest biological match. The 2026 Fayuvi approval adds a future CNS treatment-window comparator—its indication explicitly refers to preserved neurodevelopmental function—but it is outside the 2000–2025 primary period and has no mature sales history.

FDA Fayuvi approval record.

Twenty structured case dossiers

Each dossier separates contemporaneous beliefs, subsequent observations, mechanism hypotheses, competing explanations and the specific CMP model use. Cases with missing forecasts remain visible. Clinical-screen-only entries require further original-label/prior-care verification before entering a quantitative cohort.

Spinraza / SMA

first approved targeted DMTMixed by horizon; long-target miss in one forecast

Belief / classification at launch. Aug.1 2016 Needham quotation expected more than $5bn worldwide sales in 2022; this is one analyst, outside the primary vintage window.

Observed evidence. Biogen reported $1,793.5m in 2022. That is more than 64% below the quoted threshold; early launch enthusiasm is not evidence of long-run forecast accuracy.

Mechanism to test. Initial prevalent-patient conversion and loading can accelerate early revenue; competing therapies and switches can reduce later product share.

Competing explanations / limits. Do not conflate total SMA market growth with Spinraza revenue. Age, disease severity, newborn screening and intrathecal feasibility differ from SYNGAP1.

Use for CMP-002. Use a loading/maintenance cohort model, procedure capacity and competitor scenarios. Do not copy SMA efficacy or patient penetration into SYNGAP1.

What is missing. Original Needham model and primary-vintage reports; quarterly US/global patient starts, maintenance and switches; documented PoS treatment.

Needham forecast quoted August 1, 2016 by StreetInsider · Biogen 2022 Form 10-K, Spinraza revenues · Spinraza prescribing information

Brineura / CLN2

first approved DMTNot evaluable against a forecast

Belief / classification at launch. No usable original preapproval annual model recovered. An undated or unspecified peak estimate cannot be converted into a launch curve.

Observed evidence. Reported global revenues rose from about $8.6m in 2017 to $39.9m in 2018, $72.0m in 2019 and $110.192m in 2020. These calendar years do not equal first 36 commercial months.

Mechanism to test. A working delivery network and timely diagnosis can matter as much as prevalence. Preserving function and reversing lost development are distinct claims.

Competing explanations / limits. CLN2 is degenerative; SYNGAP1 developmental impairment may have a different reversible component. ICV infusion is operationally different from lumbar intrathecal dosing.

Use for CMP-002. Use center activation, visits per patient, treatment delays and age/function strata; no numerical penetration transfer without matched patient data.

What is missing. 2016–2017 original BioMarin models, forecast launch dates, quarterly early revenues and original label/center capacity history.

BioMarin 2017 Form 10-K · BioMarin 2019 Form 10-K, product revenues · BioMarin 2021 Form 10-K, product revenues

Mepsevii / MPS VII

first approved ERTNot evaluable

Belief / classification at launch. No usable annual sell-side forecast recovered; the case remains in the universe despite poor forecast access.

Observed evidence. Ultragenyx reported $0.5m product sales in 2017 including named-patient sales; the label does not establish CNS benefit.

Mechanism to test. A few patients and the timing of paid access can materially change revenue in an ultra-small cohort.

Competing explanations / limits. Free drug, compassionate access, country reimbursement and dosing by weight can mimic penetration changes. This is not evidence of a large latent population becoming commercial.

Use for CMP-002. Model counts and treated patient-months directly at the low end; retain uncertainty in genetic confirmation and access. Do not use peripheral ERT efficacy to predict neurological rescue.

What is missing. 2016/2017 annual forecasts, original risk/geography assumptions, paid versus free access and quarterly country revenue.

Ultragenyx first MPS VII approval announcement, November 15, 2017 · Ultragenyx 2017 Form 10-K · Mepsevii prescribing information

Naglazyme / MPS VI

first approved ERTRaw 2007 actual above forecast

Belief / classification at launch. January 2004 SG Cowen-authored table: $30m sales for 2007. Risk and exact geography unspecified.

Observed evidence. BioMarin reported $86.2m in 2007, including $68.7m outside the US. Raw difference +187%; US sales alone derive to $17.5m.

Mechanism to test. International rollout and patient access are candidate contributors. The contemporaneous forecast provides an earlier belief, not a complete patient/pricing model.

Competing explanations / limits. If the forecast were US-only, the apparent beat would reverse. FX added $4.3m versus 2006; expected ex-US partnering versus direct sales could change recognized revenue.

Use for CMP-002. Supports testing geographic and revenue-owner assumptions. It cannot support a universal diagnosis multiplier or SYNGAP1 CNS-response assumption.

What is missing. Underlying SG Cowen annual model, expected territory rights/launch dates, probability treatment, net price and treated patients.

Yaron Werber (SG Cowen), Lysosomal storage diseases market, January 2004 · BioMarin 2007 Form 10-K

Myozyme / Pompe

first approved ERTRaw 2007 actual above forecast

Belief / classification at launch. January 2004 SG Cowen-authored table: $65m in 2007, Phase III at publication.

Observed evidence. Genzyme reported $200.7m in 2007 versus $59.2m in 2006. Commercial rollout included multiple major territories; favorable FX contributed $4.3m versus 2006.

Mechanism to test. Earlier/wider country access, patients treated, exposure and price are candidate mechanisms.

Competing explanations / limits. Expected infant-versus-adult scope is unresolved. A later brand/manufacturing distinction with Lumizyme must not create an independent first-DMT observation.

Use for CMP-002. Separate incident early-treated children from older prevalent patients; allow efficacy, dose and reimbursement to differ. Do not assume neurological developmental rescue from muscle survival benefit.

What is missing. Original 2004/2005 model, age mix, country revenue, launch assumptions and original conditionality.

Yaron Werber (SG Cowen), Lysosomal storage diseases market, January 2004 · Genzyme 2007 annual financial statements, Exhibit 13

Elaprase / MPS II

first approved ERTRaw 2007 actual above forecast

Belief / classification at launch. January 2004 analyst table: $30m in 2007. The same article described most estimated Hunter patients as already diagnosed.

Observed evidence. Shire reported $181.8m in 2007; its discussion credits a full US year, European launches and preapproval Latin American sales.

Mechanism to test. Launch geography, conversion, dose and price deserve investigation before diagnosis expansion is credited.

Competing explanations / limits. A raw sixfold ratio can also reflect risk weighting or forecasted timing. Systemic enzyme benefit does not establish cognitive efficacy.

Use for CMP-002. Distinguish diagnosed from accessible and willing to start; do not treat every historical sales beat as newly found patients.

What is missing. Original I2S/TKT model, Shire acquisition/territory assumptions, risk basis and forecast patient counts.

Yaron Werber (SG Cowen), Lysosomal storage diseases market, January 2004 · Shire full-year 2007 financial results

Vimizim / MPS IVA

first approved ERTApproximately in line on raw target year

Belief / classification at launch. Nov.21 2013 article cited Bloomberg analysts expecting $416m annual sales in 2017, shortly before Feb.2014 approval.

Observed evidence. BioMarin reported $413.251m in 2017: raw difference −0.66%.

Mechanism to test. A reasonably anticipated market/launch is possible; not every first enzyme launch produced a large positive surprise.

Competing explanations / limits. An apparent match could conceal offsetting patient, price, geography and timing errors. Constituent analysts and risk basis remain unknown.

Use for CMP-002. Retain as a neutral countercase when reviewing the earlier ERT cluster; avoid treating commercial archetype alone as a forecast uplift.

What is missing. Bloomberg constituent models and timestamped annual consensus; approval-conditionality and geography.

Fierce Pharma, Vimizim panel nod fuels new BioMarin buyout buzz, Nov.21 2013 · BioMarin 2017 Form 10-K, Note 20 product revenues

Kanuma / LAL deficiency

first approved ERTLarge raw shortfall

Belief / classification at launch. Reuters May6 2015 cited Cortellis at $603m in 2020. The separately quoted company >$1bn ambition is not an analyst forecast.

Observed evidence. Alexion reported $117.9m in 2020: raw difference −80.45%. In 2020 it lowered long-term patient-growth assumptions and recorded a $2,042.3m intangible impairment.

Mechanism to test. The filing directly describes patient-growth difficulty and reprioritization of commercial effort. A theoretically undiagnosed population need not become a treated population.

Competing explanations / limits. The filing is retrospective and does not decompose the analyst gap. Territory, net price, pandemic disruption and original risk basis remain unresolved.

Use for CMP-002. Model diagnosis as a funded operational process with conversion losses, not a percentage that rises automatically. Include a slow-finding downside.

What is missing. Cortellis underlying forecast contributors, annual curves, geography/PoS; original patient-finding assumptions and longitudinal diagnosis-to-start conversion.

Reuters May 6 2015, Alexion to buy Synageva · Alexion 2020 Form 10-K · Alexion 2020 Form 10-K, intangible assets note

Luxturna / RPE65 retinal dystrophy

first approved gene therapy for indicationNot evaluable

Belief / classification at launch. No matched preapproval annual curve recovered. Disease prevalence is broader than eligible viable-retina patients.

Observed evidence. Spark reported $27.0m net product revenue in 2018; contract revenue is separate. Later ownership and territory rights complicate a consistent series.

Mechanism to test. Procedure readiness, tissue eligibility and reimbursement can restrict conversion even with a causal therapy.

Competing explanations / limits. One-time backlog depletion and royalty accounting differ from repeat ASO revenue. Limited public sales disclosure is not proof of weak demand.

Use for CMP-002. Use an eligibility and center-capacity funnel, but do not import one-time prices or cumulative-sales shape into CMP-002.

What is missing. Original 2016–2017 forecasts, territory-specific end-market sales after acquisition, procedures/patient and original tissue criteria.

FDA LUXTURNA product approval record · Spark 2018 Form 10-K

Nulibry / MoCD-A

first approved replacement DMTNot evaluable

Belief / classification at launch. Prior investigational cPMP exposure must inform launch conversion but does not automatically disqualify a first approved therapy.

Observed evidence. Original label targets mortality risk and daily IV administration; it permits initiation for presumed disease with genetic confirmation required.

Mechanism to test. Timing of diagnosis can determine clinical opportunity, not merely how quickly a commercially eligible backlog is captured.

Competing explanations / limits. A neonatal survival window is much narrower than the currently studied SYNGAP1 age range. Severe historical mortality changes prevalent-pool arithmetic.

Use for CMP-002. Use to challenge assumptions about reversible function and incident versus prevalent patients; no transferable penetration percentage is established.

What is missing. 2019–2021 fosdenopterin/BridgeBio models, paid-versus-investigational patients, incident diagnoses and reported product economics.

Nulibry original FDA label, 2021

Lenmeldy / MLD

prior-HSCT boundary; first approved gene therapyImmature

Belief / classification at launch. Original approval in March2024 cannot yield 36 commercial months by this study date.

Observed evidence. Indication specifies presymptomatic late-infantile, presymptomatic early-juvenile or early-symptomatic early-juvenile children.

Mechanism to test. Earlier genetic identification may expand the treatable window rather than unlock all older diagnosed patients.

Competing explanations / limits. HSCT precedent and ex-vivo manufacturing prevent clean first-treatment or operational equivalence to CMP-002.

Use for CMP-002. Build age/function eligibility explicitly; keep developmental benefit separate from symptom control.

What is missing. Preapproval Orchard/Kyowa models, initial-label eligible population, manufactured/infused patients and territory timing.

FDA LENMELDY product approval record

Kebilidi / AADC deficiency

first approved targeted US candidate; prior-care sensitivityImmature

Belief / classification at launch. Approval November2024 is too recent for the primary sales horizon. Earlier symptomatic/neurotransmitter care requires explicit review.

Observed evidence. FDA indicates adult and pediatric AADC deficiency. No mature cumulative accuracy estimate is available here.

Mechanism to test. Residual developmental capacity and specialist procedural delivery are clinically relevant hypotheses.

Competing explanations / limits. Gene addition to specific brain regions, one-time surgery and AADC biology differ from repeat regRNA-targeting ASO therapy.

Use for CMP-002. Use efficacy and delivery dimensions separately; do not convert motor milestone data into a SYNGAP1 commercial response rate.

What is missing. PTC preapproval annual models, original label/review, country-specific treatment starts, reimbursement and procedure capacity.

FDA KEBILIDI product approval record

Myalept / Generalized lipodystrophy

first replacement candidate; prior metabolic careNot evaluable

Belief / classification at launch. A broader lipodystrophy population cannot be equated with the US generalized-disease market.

Observed evidence. Aegerion disclosed approximately 30% 2015 US gross-to-net adjustments in a preliminary update, with 27 percentage points attributable to Medicaid rebates.

Mechanism to test. Payer mix and price history can dominate WAC-to-revenue conversion even in a rare replacement therapy.

Competing explanations / limits. This single-product, historical estimate is not a contemporary ASO discount benchmark; rights transfers and accounting also matter.

Use for CMP-002. Model net revenue by payer and territory; use 15% or another GTN only as a justified CMP assumption, not an orphan-drug constant.

What is missing. 2013–2014 Amylin/BMS/AstraZeneca analyst models, original population scope and payer/price assumptions.

Aegerion 2015 Form 10-K · Aegerion Jan.11 2016 preliminary FY2015 update

Arcalyst / CAPS

first approved disease-targeted candidate with prior off-label blockadeNot evaluable

Belief / classification at launch. Prior off-label anakinra and subsequent Ilaris prevent a simple untreated-to-monopoly narrative.

Observed evidence. Regeneron reported $6.3m, $18.4m and $25.3m in 2008–2010; 2010 included $4.8m of deferred sales recognition.

Mechanism to test. Accounting and conversion from free/clinical supply can distort revenue ramps independently of new patient demand.

Competing explanations / limits. Later pericarditis revenue must not be attributed to CAPS. Different efficacy and administration choices affect competition.

Use for CMP-002. Separate starts, paid treatment and recognized revenue; keep competitors in the adoption model.

What is missing. 2007 Regeneron/Arcalyst annual models, deferred-revenue schedule and CAPS-only sales through later indications.

Regeneron 2010 annual report

Kuvan / PKU

boundary: prior disease-modifying dietNot evaluable

Belief / classification at launch. Diet already altered disease course, so first approved drug does not mean first DMT under a care-inclusive definition.

Observed evidence. Candidate retained to test whether response-defined eligibility and established care make apparent TAM misleading; no new forecast error estimated.

Mechanism to test. Biochemical responsiveness and willingness to change existing treatment can be more important than prevalence.

Competing explanations / limits. Early-screened PKU diagnosis economics are unlike underdiagnosed SYNGAP1; oral administration is easier than intrathecal dosing.

Use for CMP-002. Model biologically responsive and clinically eligible fractions separately from diagnosis and penetration.

What is missing. Original 2006–2007 sapropterin forecasts, response-testing assumptions and BioMarin/Merck territory economics; verify original care evidence before quantitative use.

FDA CDER original approval compilation, 1985–2025

Dojolvi / LC-FAOD

boundary: prior diet/MCT and compassionate triheptanoinNot evaluable

Belief / classification at launch. First approved formulation differs from first available disease-targeted intervention.

Observed evidence. Retained despite lack of a new matched annual forecast; initial-label and exposure adjudication remains open.

Mechanism to test. Conversion of existing users and payer willingness to reimburse an approved product can shape launch revenue.

Competing explanations / limits. Switching from prior nutritional care is distinct from creating access to a new CNS mechanism. Adherence metrics may have different meaning.

Use for CMP-002. Track previously treated, free-access and genuinely new patients separately.

What is missing. 2019–2020 Ultragenyx models, paid transition counts and original-label dietary comparator; verify clinical first-status boundary.

FDA CDER original approval compilation, 1985–2025

Ztalmy / CDKL5 deficiency disorder

adjacent disease-specific antiseizure treatmentNot evaluable

Belief / classification at launch. An antiseizure approval should not be reclassified as developmental disease modification. Multi-indication ganaxolone forecasts are excluded.

Observed evidence. Marinus disclosed $19.6m US net revenue in 2023 and >165 active patients at year-end; it described greater use of third-party data for patient targeting.

Mechanism to test. Specialist reach and caregiver-supported initiation are observable commercial processes; patient lists are not treated patients.

Competing explanations / limits. Company targeting counts are not validated prevalence. Annual revenue divided by a year-end count is not annual net price per patient.

Use for CMP-002. Use diagnosis-to-referral-to-prescription-to-start-to-persistence transitions, with explicit oral versus procedure burden.

What is missing. 2021/early2022 CDD-only models, competing indication allocations, paid patient-months, discontinuation and GTN.

Marinus March 2024 commercial update filed with SEC · Marinus November 2024 commercial update

Daybue / Rett

adjacent disease-specific therapy; DMT uncertainNot evaluable on primary endpoint

Belief / classification at launch. First Rett approval is not equivalent to proof of genetic disease modification. Existing analyst peak quotations do not provide a cumulative curve.

Observed evidence. Reported product revenue was $177.2m in 2023, $348.4m in 2024 and $391.4m in 2025. Initiation, tolerability and sustained use need separate evidence.

Mechanism to test. The early prevalent backlog and later continuation may drive different phases of the curve.

Competing explanations / limits. New territories, formulation changes and follow-up length can confound apparent persistence. Oral experience cannot fix intrathecal willingness.

Use for CMP-002. Model starts and continuing treated patient-months separately; do not apply a single adherence factor twice.

What is missing. Preapproval 2022–2023 curves, label/geography assumptions, cohort continuation with consistent denominators and paid dispensing.

FDA: first treatment for Rett syndrome · Daybue original FDA label, 2023 · Acadia full-year 2024 results · Acadia full-year 2025 results

Miplyffa / NPC

boundary: prior miglustat / same-era new entrantsImmature

Belief / classification at launch. First US NPC approval does not erase earlier European/off-label miglustat treatment.

Observed evidence. September2024 original approval is too recent for 36-month actuals; Aqneursa followed within days.

Mechanism to test. Combination treatment and close competition make product share distinct from disease-level treatment uptake.

Competing explanations / limits. Cross-country care and labeling differ; pooling all NPC brands would change the unit of analysis.

Use for CMP-002. Maintain a disease-market model and a separate CMP share model; no assumption of permanent first-mover exclusivity.

What is missing. Preapproval NPC-only curves, original label and prior-care sources, combination costs and competitor switches.

FDA CDER original approval compilation, 1985–2025

Qalsody / SOD1 ALS

boundary: prior ALS DMTs; genotype-targeted ASONot evaluable

Belief / classification at launch. First SOD1-directed therapy differs from first ALS DMT. Biomarker evidence and demonstrated functional benefit must remain separate.

Observed evidence. 2023 approval allows only limited mature follow-up; this pass does not recover a matched cumulative curve or separate reported product economics.

Mechanism to test. Diagnostic genotype confirmation, uncertain perceived benefit and repeated procedures can affect adoption.

Competing explanations / limits. Adult ALS survival and motor-neuron disease are distant from childhood SYNGAP1 developmental trajectories.

Use for CMP-002. Use route/modality operational questions and surrogate-versus-clinical evidence gates; do not transfer ALS survival or uptake.

What is missing. 2022/early2023 tofersen models, conditionality, paid patient exposure and original accelerated-approval evidence.

FDA CDER original approval compilation, 1985–2025

What this means for CMP-002

CMP-002 is a repeat intrathecal ASO intended to raise SYNGAP1 expression through regulatory-RNA targeting. As of the November5 2026 company update, first patient dosing in ASCEND was planned for year-end; human clinical efficacy is not yet established. It should be called a potential first DMT, with first-at-launch status and disease-modifying benefit treated as hypotheses.

The September28 2026 trial design includes ages 2 to under18, specified truncating mutations in exons5–19, and substantial seizure-related entry criteria. Those are trial eligibility rules, not an approved future label or the whole disease prevalence. Missense/nontruncating variants, microdeletions and other excluded groups cannot be silently counted as proven commercial candidates. Conversely, the eventual label should not be assumed permanently identical to the first trial.

Current SYNGAP1 care includes antiseizure medicines, rescue treatment, selected dietary/device approaches and developmental support. Thus, first DMT would enter an existing clinical and caregiver ecosystem. Incremental value must be assessed across seizures and broader development/function rather than treating the population as wholly untreated.

CAMP4 November5 2026 update · September28 2026 SEC trial-design disclosure · CHOP SYNGAP1 care overview.

The patient-organization publication reports a March31 2024 census of 1,400 known patients worldwide and discusses 401 counted in the US. These are dated ascertainment counts, not 2026 prevalence. It also describes a modeled de novo incidence of 6.107 per100,000 births, composed of 0.787 truncating and 5.320 missense variants. The mismatch between modeled variant incidence and the diagnosed cohort is unresolved. Total predicted incidence therefore cannot be multiplied by population and treated as the eligible market for a trial focused on specified truncating variants.

Stoke's August2026 update says lead optimization was underway for a SYNGAP1 clinical candidate. It is direct evidence that eventual first-mover status and competitive durability cannot be assumed. The current review is not an exhaustive competitor census. Likewise, the primary trial-design source used here is CAMP4's SEC disclosure; an official trial-registry record was not independently reconciled in this pass.

SYNGAP1 patient-organization census and incidence discussion · Stoke August3 2026 pipeline update.

The commercial model should expose six separate mechanisms

Model componentUpside hypothesisDisconfirming observation / downsideHistorical guide
Findable patient poolBetter genetic testing identifies previously unrecognized eligible patients.Low diagnostic yield, inaccessible records, noneligible genotypes or few referrals despite testing.Kanuma; Ztalmy; Mepsevii
Clinical eligibility and benefitMeaningful benefit extends across age/function groups.Older or severe patients have little reversible benefit; evidence/label narrows scope.Lenmeldy; Nulibry; Luxturna; Myozyme
Initiation and capacitySpecialist networks convert identified patients efficiently.Procedure slots, travel, sedation or caregiver burden delay starts.Spinraza; Brineura; Kebilidi
Exposure and continuationPatients sustain meaningful benefit and continue treatment.Interruptions, discontinuation, missed procedures or perceived limited benefit reduce paid exposure.Daybue; Ztalmy; repeated CNS therapies
Net economics and territoriesPayer access and international rollout support value capture.Rebates, free drug, lower country prices, rights structure or delayed reimbursement erode revenue.Myalept; Naglazyme; Elaprase
Competitive durabilityFirst entrant remains preferred with a durable clinical advantage.New routes/mechanisms shift starts or switch existing patients.Spinraza; Arcalyst; NPC boundary cases
Paid revenue in a period = Σ (treated patient-months × net revenue per treated month)
Patient starts are constrained by eligibility, referral, willingness, reimbursement and procedure capacity.
Continuing exposure follows cohort-specific discontinuation, interruptions and survival.

This avoids multiplying a single prevalence figure by several overlapping “penetration,” “adherence” and “persistence” discounts. Separate newly diagnosed patients from an existing backlog and incident births. Model each territory's launch/reimbursement date and economic rights. Do not transplant enzyme weight-based pricing into an ASO without a dosing rationale.

The supplied Needham model already assumes diagnosis rises from 15% to 50%, 50% penetration of diagnosed patients, 75% adherence/persistence and 15% GTN; its $2.106bn conditional peak has 25% PoS applied in valuation. Therefore, “analysts omit diagnosis expansion” is not an adequate critique of that model. The independent task is to test whether the timing, denominator, eligible fractions and continuation assumptions are right. These Street assumptions remain context, not inputs used to choose this historical cohort.

Historical evidence can enter the model now as structural constraints and named upside/downside scenarios. It does not yet justify an empirically calibrated penetration, GTN or persistence uplift. Preclinical functional rescue cannot fill that gap. The separate reverse-DCF project should compare the market-implied commercial curve with a separately built epidemiology model, while allowing financing, development costs, dilution, timing and other pipeline value to explain a gap. A sales gap alone is not proof of undervaluation.

Street assumptions are documented in the prior source-verified CAMP evidence package and the preserved Needham report; no new valuation is calculated here.

Which investor claims are defensible?

ClaimAssessmentEvidence needed to strengthen it
“Analysts systematically underforecast first rare-disease DMTs.”Not established. The available comparisons are nonrepresentative and not risk/scope matched.A reconciled universe and timestamped original annual curves with known conditionality.
“Several early ERT sales forecasts were substantially below subsequently reported sales.”Supported as a qualified raw historical observation from one original analyst publication.Underlying model geography, PoS, price and patient assumptions; independent analysts for the same cases.
“A first therapy automatically unlocks the undiagnosed population.”Not supported; Kanuma is direct disconfirming operational evidence.Longitudinal tests → diagnoses → eligible referrals → starts, with cost and time per conversion.
“A diagnosed population can still expand commercially through territory/access changes.”Plausible and supported as a mechanism; magnitude not isolated.Country patient/exposure data and the original forecast rollout schedule.
“First DMT and clinical benefit make later competitors unimportant.”Not supported. Product share and disease-market growth are different.Competitor timing, relative benefit, switching and administration preferences.
“CMP-002 has underappreciated upside if diagnosis, eligibility or durable benefit exceeds current assumptions.”A valid conditional hypothesis, not a demonstrated valuation conclusion.Independent epidemiology, genotype/function segmentation, human evidence, access/capacity economics and market-implied curve.

A defensible narrative would say: historical first rare-disease launches show that commercial outcomes depend on identifiable assumptions about the reachable population, treatment exposure and access. Some early replacement-therapy estimates were too low; other first therapies disappointed. CMP-002's potential upside should be demonstrated through those specific assumptions and supported by human evidence, rather than asserted from rarity or first-mover status.

Highest-value missing data and stopping rule

The bottleneck is original annual forecast models and matching fields, not another collection of peak-sales headlines. No outreach has been initiated. The following is a concrete acquisition specification for existing institutional access or later authorized collection.

PriorityExact requestWhy it has high information value
1 — resolve the new clusterSG Cowen / Yaron Werber LSD models underlying Jan.2004 Table1; BioMarin/Naglazyme, Genzyme/Myozyme and TKT/I2S coverage. Retrieve annual revenue, territory, patient, price, launch and PoS equations.Three authentic preapproval observations share a source; one set of original assumptions can resolve whether the strongest apparent beats survive normalization.
2 — recover independent same-case forecastsFor Naglazyme (approval May31 2005), Myozyme (Apr28 2006) and Elaprase (Jul24 2006), obtain each bank’s nearest 9–15-month preapproval model, then latest preapproval model.Separates one analyst/early-vintage cluster from a reproducible disease-level pattern. Do not select the lowest estimate.
3 — balance with neutral/downside casesBloomberg constituent Vimizim models behind Nov21 2013 $416m/2017; Cortellis contributors and model behind May6 2015 Kanuma $603m/2020; also their primary-vintage snapshots.Resolves source composition and geography with symmetric retrieval rather than searching only successful drugs.
4 — match closest CNS analoguesSpinraza 2015–2016 and Brineura 2016–2017 full annual models; identify original forecasts before pivotal results separately.Most relevant to repeated CNS treatment; obtain starts, loading/maintenance, eligible age and procedure assumptions.
5 — identify clinic and net-price mechanismsCDD-only Ztalmy 2021/early2022; Rett-only Daybue 2022/early2023; Myalept 2013–2014; Mepsevii 2016–2017.Separate neurodevelopmental adoption, tolerability, tiny-population dynamics and GTN without requiring all to be strict DMTs.
6 — complete the sampling denominatorUsable OOPD approved-drug export, non-NME/supplement reconciliation and historical CBER biologics/withdrawal records.Needed before claiming a representative universe or estimating a population bias.
7 — observable operational validationDeidentified aggregate testing yield from genetic labs; registry enrollment and genotype/age counts; center procedure throughput; specialty-pharmacy paid exposure; payer mix and authorization delays.These observers measure the actual conversion process. No patient-level data or outreach is assumed authorized.

Required fields for each forecast: exact timestamp, analyst/bank, original model or page, drug and disease, geographic rights, nominal currency, annual net-sales curve, launch assumptions, population/price/exposure drivers, label/age/genotype scope and where PoS enters. Obtain corresponding monthly/quarterly actuals, country splits, free-drug treatment, licensing revenue and disclosure changes. Keep failed retrievals as missing observations.

After acquisition, predefine equal-weight drug-level aggregation and rerun first-status, vintage, geography, unchanged-label and leave-one-source-family-out sensitivities. If scope cannot be reconciled, retain the case as mechanism evidence. Do not fit a pooled regression to a handful of heterogeneous excerpts. The current public-source pass stops here because additional generic searching is unlikely to repair the missing model fields.

Deliverables, checks and reproducibility

Arithmetic was recomputed from stored numbers; frame counts and source references were reconciled; no unknown PoS was filled; every original 2004 table row was retained; and every new comparison remains excluded from the strict cumulative sample. These checks establish internal consistency, not independent validation of clinical similarity or causal attribution.

  • Structured case evidence, source registry and data dictionary
  • Forecast/actual comparison CSV
  • Analogue matrix CSV
  • Full approval-screen CSV
  • 1,442-row regulatory disposition ledger
  • 99-entry extended candidate universe
  • Recomputed counts, arithmetic and matching checks
  • Explicit scope and access limitations
  • Regulatory originals, hashes and failed-export status
  • Outcome-independent universe reconstruction
  • Curated source extraction and case records
Data dictionary
FieldMeaning
unitDrug–indication pair; raw frame rows are approvals/products, not independent forecast outcomes.
classificationProvisional clinical scope/first-status screen; does not certify scientific DMT status.
source_locatorWorksheet row or document table/section; raw source preserved where feasible.
nullUnknown/not obtained, never zero.
forecast_usdmObserved quoted nominal USD millions, without imputed PoS correction.
actual_usdmObserved recognized net product revenue; consolidated worldwide unless specified.
raw_actual_over_forecast_minus_1Derived A/F−1; positive means raw actual exceeds quoted forecast. Not normalized forecast bias.
break_even_scalar_posHypothetical F/A where F=p×conditional forecast; not an estimate of actual approval probability.
strict_36m_eligibleRequires complete matched first 36 commercial months, original disease, geography, revenue basis, currency, vintage and known conditionality.
dimensionsInvestigator qualitative relevance on biology, age/window, diagnosis, administration and exposure; no outcome-fitted composite score.
commercial_directionUnknown unless target-specific evidence exists. Immaturity/access gaps are not commercial failures.
source_reuseMarked where earlier verified evidence is used after independent case selection.
knowledge_timePreapproval forecast beliefs are separated from subsequent company explanations and investigator hypotheses.
